Conclusion: TG1 & TG2 reached. Hindsight: This trade very quickly showed I could have doubled the trade reward by waiting longer. I can think of various tasks for my to- do list. This could be the catalyst to collect statistics on taking different type of trade exits. There is always a decision to be made at each stage of the trade. Entries. Active trade period. News events. Exits. Perhaps in this case I could have employed a trailing stop. Or waited for PA in the opposite direction. Sorry guys just thinking out loud here. Expectation vs Anticipation vs Doubt vs Cold Hard Statistics! <--- This is what I want to build. Sometimes I feel you can spend so much time trying to find that perfect entry and when you finally get it, it doesn't end there, your guard is a little down, and then you realise the tough battle to fight when it comes to exits. The following user(s) said Thank You: ShadowPit, cuimingzhao 11/39 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=225 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 10 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU ShadowPit #234 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 I am not too scientific about chart or trade selection as there are so many opportunities on every chart every day. When I first sit down I will click through around 20 pairs 1-by- 1, pretty quickly, and eyeball the charts. Usually a chart will simply pop out to me before I get to the end of the 20 pairs and I'll drill deeper. For example, in the chart above. It was a very common and obvious price setup which I am used to at a glance.: 1. There was a strong push down creating a pole. 2. In the middle of that sharp move down you can see the FL formed at the break of the low on the left. 3. You can observe clearly the price retracing and "engulfing" the FL to the left which forms the signal to go long at the decision point and/or QM level. 4. The only thing I had left to do was check that price was coming from a HTF demand and that was that. This is a very common setup that occurs within a few hours after high impact news events. It ain't always that easy though, the majority of other trade setups I observe and take usually require far deeper investigation, they can often be more complex and not conforming to any specific price setup model like the one described above, they can also be time consuming to come to a decision on direction, and then require a lot of https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=225 12/39 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 10 - RTM Community waiting for the right level, but this can all be managed with alerts, which I do use. But HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT yeah, I hold no allegiance to any one pair or currency, but from my behaviour of scanning through 1-by-1 I usually end up trading the same pairs! Unfortunately I don't really have any qualified advice for you. Given your fatigue, I might suggest you at least start small and note down 1-3 different setups you are comfortable with, like the one I have described above, and work on identifying those setups on 4 pairs only and pick those charts apart over the course of a week and reassess on the weekend whether you need more or not. Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU EURUSD ideas #266 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 Hello, Firstly, I do not know where it will retrace to, nor do I create any expectations on where it will go. Secondly, that engulf you have marked off doesn't really tell you much, it also doesn't tell the same story on D1 charts, which suggests price has bounced from D1 demand. In regards to the fakeout you have marked, if you look on the daily its a perfect touch on a previous resistance area, so take from that what you will...Be careful with how you label things on your chart, you have labeled things on your chart which in combination give you conflicting bias on where to trade from and where to. Ok so onto what I think about the EURUSD: EURUSD H4 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=250 26/41 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT EURUSD M15 If I were to short this chart the blue rectangle is where I would look for PA on the H4. ** 27/41 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=250 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 11 - RTM Community I am interested in approach, because if price enters that area it will quite possibly engulf HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT the FL with the green circle outline. If price creates demand when it breaks this FL I would be interested in seeing that demand engulfed before looking for short opportunities on the LTF. If I were to touch trade a short, I would be looking for price to approach the maroon area on the M15 chart. Ideally a compressed approach. Some things I would be cautious about if price reaches this area include what price looks like on the Weekly/Daily charts and if it is congruent with the LTF analysis, I would ask myself what this means for my trade targets and where I can logically take profit off or hold further. **Conversely if this area were to break I would look to find any long opportunities. When you create some sort of a plan like the one above, you begin to anticipate where price will go (targets), and when your targets are correct more often than not, you begin to take trades in the opposite direction you're anticipating as price has not yet reached its target. So the other question you should be asking yourself is, how do I get in long, and where should I get out? It will ultimately come down to the other areas of your trading plan such as your profit objectives, risk management, how long you're willing to wait for price to reach your area, and how long you're willing to hold. Whenever - Sometimes I will draw a line of best fit through S/R based on how price is reacting at a level and my risk appetite. I recommend you don't follow this (this is simply how I am tuned to the market, how I pinpoint the imbalance in the supply and demand equation). The logic is based on how price reacted specifically to those lines, I asked questions like where did price originate from when it tanked, where couldn't the cluster close above, where couldn't the cluster close below. The entry was based on minimising risk as much as possible, and getting in where price couldn't previously close above on this timeframe. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=300 9/33 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 13 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Experiorz, whynotnaveen, cuimingzhao 10/33 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=300 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 13 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU GBPJPY #310 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 Some post GBPJPY analysis. The following user(s) said Thank You: ShadowPit QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU ShadowPit #313 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 Thanks whynotnaveen, There is absolutely nothing wrong with 20pip stop losses or 2.5R as you're learning about trading and getting comfortable with what works for YOU! If you were to critically review your trading you just might find out some interesting things about your decisions and trading behaviour. It just might be possible to extract https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=300 15/33 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 13 - RTM Community more from your trades than you realise, perhaps you're always entering too early (touch HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT trade large zones)...or too late (chasing price after its reacted). In the right context a 20 pip stop loss could mean a 200 pip win if using a D1 zone but M15/M30 precision entries, that's 10:1! Or perhaps you're not drilling down enough into the zone and discovering the more precise entry with a tighter stop loss 10-15 pips, both on the entry and exit, which could potentially turn your 2.5R into a 4R trade. There are a lot of factors you have control over :) The power of self review should not be underestimated. Good luck! I mean, how you start your trading morning/day? Mine is always chaotic when I was monitoring multiple pairs (10 pairs). How did you start your day and still could scalp beautifully, when you are monitoring more than 15 (or 20)? Big salute for your Monday trades. I've always thought Monday is a bad day for trade. I'm not confidence even with London session as I thought the ITs are still sleeping I know you didn't direct this at me, but I also monitor a large number of pairs - the way I manage it is as follows: - I always only ever have one chart at a time fullscreened - I condense my view (zoom out a lot) - you need to be able to see as much context as possible at all times, to get a feel for the big picture - widescreen monitors are not good for this, it squashes all the PA too much vertically. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=300 25/33 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 13 - RTM Community Either use a monitor with a more square aspect ratio, or use the fixed chart scale option HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT a lot to stretch things out vertically - the more vertically exaggerated the PA, the easier it is to read - I cycle through each chart first thing in the morning on M15/H1, and mark the nearest levels that I want to look for trades at above and below where price currently is - you MUST use alerts if you want to trade this many instruments. Many times, it's just a case of marking the best levels above/below price for each chart. setting alerts, and then just waiting for price to reach them. The main benefit to trading this many instruments is that you can trade only the more significant levels without too much waiting in between. Something usually hits something halfway significant every hour or two - I either play some computer game whilst I wait, or I just randomly click through each chart if I am bored, and cycle timeframes, to see if there are any scalps available that catch my eye The following user(s) said Thank You: ShadowPit, classicj, kxfx, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU ShadowPit #321 kxfx Offline Trader Posts: 57 Thank you received: 79 Sir ShadowPit, Thanks!! I get that it helps some folk understand the basics of price action but ultimately they're all just made up and don't contain the necessary information for me to provide a qualified response, I never look at one chart and make a Go/No go decision. I like to drill down...and zoom out....flick between 2-4 time frames, look at correlations. I like to know what's happening in the bigger picture. Where is price coming from, where is it going to. What other levels are there that price has turned from. That's just the way I operate, so I cannot give you a definitive answer to your question nor will I attempt one. Look at it this way, if you're confused with your current understanding of a concept, anything I or anyone tells you one way or the other will not give you the fool proof answer to apply to the same future scenarios for you to profit 100% of the time, so there's no point in me telling you what I would do....my recommendation comes in the way of these three choices: 1. Price has violated your original plan, you do nothing, you move on to your next opportunity. 2. Price has violated your original plan, but PA is providing an opportunity in your original desired direction, it fits within your risk profile, so you just take the trade and see what happens. That's what taking risks is about. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=325 7/32 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 14 - RTM Community 3. Price has violated your original plan, but PA is providing an opportunity opposite to HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT your original desired direction, it fits within your risk profile, so you just take the trade and see what happens. That's what taking risks is about. Over time you're going to gain a much better understanding through just giving trades a go and answering your own questions with empirical evidence from your own trading data. The following user(s) said Thank You: ShadowPit, cuimingzhao https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=325 8/32 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 14 - RTM Community QUICKHROEPMLYE REPELYDUCQAUTOITOEN FORUM MORE LOGOUT THANK YOU ShadowPit #332 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Just to add my 2 cents (I hope you don't mind Shadow): Origins and their engulfs are not a "rule". Really, there are no 100% rules in the market. There are just things which we can expect to happen more often than they do not when we put together all of the information at our disposal. In actuality, MPL of an origin of a major swing or FTR just represents a good *area* to look for a trade. A lot of the time, the origin gets FO'd into the space between origin MPL and the swing/FTR MPL. This is actually one of my favourite places to look for a trade - if the origin is in the form of a very prominent "hook", I will trade the very pip edge of it, expecting a FO into the space aforementioned. There is usually excellent RR available with low pip risk, if you fancy taking it. You're close, but you need to be really specific about what each attribute means for you to exploit the inefficiency of the market you're in. It's very important to dedicate the time to really absorb the teachings of the QM article and relevant examples in the Homework section and spend a lot of time concentrating and observing the key attributes that make up the pattern, once you sequentially identify each attribute, it is impossible to get it wrong. I guarantee you will get a firm grip of the form of the pattern if you can label the key attributes below, if you have to force the pattern or cross your eyes to see a QM setup to label the chart, you best move on to another chart or timeframe. Also whilst you're at it, in the process, don't forget to start asking questions why the pattern occurs, what is the psychological makeup that allows this trick to be performed time and time again? Who's buying and who's selling at each step of the process? You can then even start exploring why Diamonds begin to occur, how is that a HHH or LLL will occur, what are the factors that contribute to that extra push? So what are the key attributes for a sell QM? 1. Left shoulder (a high) 2. New demand (low) 3. The head (higher-high) 4. An engulf of the new demand (lower-low) - for higher probability you want a good surge in price, a break with a bit of conviction! 5. A retracement of price to around the left shoulder or nice supply structure around the https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=350 8/29 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 15 - RTM Community area (anticipating a lower-high) HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Once you understand the key framework and can apply it to the chart you can begin to fine tune the trade by combining the zone with confluencing factors and other recognisable patterns on LTFs such as FLs or DBDs. So in the best interest of actually getting a handle of this beautiful pattern and remaining in the present, please do the following for me (just for now): ignore the left of the chart, ignore the bigger picture and simply work to observe a decent quantity of them. At this stage of your learning. I recommend you forget about looking for them on the daily charts and get down on the M1/M5 charts and start mapping them out, they happen fast and they happen frequently (at least once or twice a session on each pair). Once you get the hang of retrospectively identifying them, demo trade the darn things real time! There is no substitute for first hand experience of anticipation. Don't spend too much time backtesting historical data. Forward test your ability to recognise the pattern in real time, you will only ever learn what to do through stepping yourself through the process over and over again. Wait for the left shoulder, wait for the higher high, wait for the engulf, place a limit order for the right shoulder, place your stop beyond the head, and target an obvious fresh zone. Forget about R:R at this stage. Trade management and fine tuning will come with time. Here are a couple of cherry picked QM patterns on AUDUSD from the last few days..Nothing particularly special about them except for the fact that they happened 'in space', outside of some consolidation or range to the left of the chart which you cannot see. The following user(s) said Thank You: cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU ShadowPit #370 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Hey buddy, hope you're well. Was having a little play with your equity spreadsheet - great little tool, thanks for 25/29 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=350 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 15 - RTM Community sharing it. EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME I think however I've noticed a couple of slight issues, which I've amended (sorry, my inner autistic geek coming out here). The formula used for calculating the result of the trade: =IF(RAND()<=$G$10,B3*(1+$G$4),IF(RAND()>$G$9,B3*(1+$G$5),B3*1)) is actually using 2 different random numbers, instead of using the same one for both parts of the comparison check, which is skewing the results somewhat. Instead, if you generate a random number in column A, and use it for both parts of the calculation, it will give a more accurate result I believe. Having made this change, I think the test for the result should become: IF WIN THEN PROFIT ELSE IF LOSS THEN -PROFIT ELSE B/E where "IF LOSS" is defined by the result being "NOT WIN AND NOT/B/E", making the new formula: =IF(A4<=$G$10,B3*(1+$G$4),IF(A4>$G$8+$G$9,B3*(1+$G$5),B3*1)) If you compare the before and after versions: I would expect to see after 1000 trades, very approximately, 40% win / 40% b/e / 20% loss, but that's somewhat different than what is being produced. After the changes: 26/29 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=350 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 15 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The figures tally a bit more as you'd expect. The change is more noticeable when you have a higher b/e rate than 5% (I only noticed this since my most successful strategy has been aiming for 1-2R or majority "not loss" with large risk on high probability reaction areas). I also introduced a "Max Consec Loss" calculated value so you can work out your maximal drawdown based on your risk. Anyway as I said, really don't want to come across as being unnecessarily critical, it's a very useful tool which I'm going to get mileage out of, but I guess even though I quit IT contracting 2 years ago, I still can't quite shake off old habits! I've attached the updated version. Please point out if I've missed something, or am wrong in my assumptions. Cheers pal, all the best. Let's look. Assuming you're not in a trade already... Let's do this backwards from LTF to HTF. AUDNZD H1 What a mess. Engulf of of lower and upper parts of the range. No point marking all the FTRs/DBR/RBD here. All Demand and Supply are taken out, and the only thing we can do at this point is trade to the hard right of the chart and wait for the decision to be made and hope for a cheap entry with that momentum. Purple box is the current range. My plan a would be for a break north and a retest of this price action area. Guess what plan b is? A break south and a retest of this price action area. Plan C. Well that's to be confirmed as price unfolds... Buy the bottom of the range, and sell the top right? Well I want to be buying the BOTTOM the NEW range and selling the TOP of the NEW range on this chart, which you might argue is buying the top and selling the bottom of some other time frame. All I know is that trading isn't as straight forward as one sentence catch phrases. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=25 30/39 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT AUDNZD D1 Now you can see why a decision needs to be made. . Most recent supply/demand areas marked off. Let's see what happens this week. Watch the price action closely at the high and at the low of last weeks candle. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU ShadowPit #62 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 juniormaiafx wrote: Just look that... is that right??? Welcome, juniormaiafx. That is not a QM. AUDUSD is in a tight range (check the weekly). A decision needs to be made one way or the other. Unfortunately your analysis will not be a reliable indicator for price reversal.. If you think you feel ready to learn QMs, I would recommend you focus on finding QM's that happen on LTF. There are LOTS of them. The best QM's to learn from will be very https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=50 17/43 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 3 - RTM Community obvious and have a form of symmetry about them. Please re-read the article and HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT observe these things about QM price: 1. The location at which the pattern occurs 2. The symmetry of the pattern 3. The momentum of each engulf 4. The approach 5. The reaction 6. What causes the low to form, what causes the high to form, what causes the lower low to form, what causes the higher high to form? (and visa versa for the opposite setup) 7. How far does price go as a result of the setup? After you do this, find in historical charts price patterns that look almost exactly like the examples. There's more than meets the eye. What I think you really should do very thoroughly FIRST is to make sure you've done all your homework. Get comfortable identifying supply and demand, support, resistance, rbd, dbr, ftr, fl, dbd, rbr. Know all that stuff like the back of your hand. While you're feeling confident you are now an expert at marking up your chart with supply/demand etc., be sure to look up the definition of the word engulf. Think about what it means in regards to trading. Dream about it daily! I do. Why does price engulf anything? What does that actually mean? Apply it to MULTIPLE time frames. One engulf may not be greater than the other. I want you to keep coming back to this forum for advice but please make sure you do the homework. Practice makes perfect. Good luck friend. When I zoomed in to the 4hr chart, I noticed a clean, strong 4hr DP to push below the htf support line, and thus, I found it fitting to put a sell limit there with the TP at daily demand. (Note, the actual order is not pictured in the charts because it was right next to another pending order and looked messy, so I just used lines to represent the order..) Now I placed my sell limit before the area of daily demand was hit with the expectation of my 4hr DP being the thing that was going to drive price down to this htf demand area. Now here comes the tricky part.. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=50 29/43 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 3 - RTM Community The question that has plagued me for quite some time is should you leave a limit order HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT in place after your TP line has been hit??? When I think about it logically, it seems that since the area of daily/ htf demand was hit, this could invalidate my sell limit since htf SD holds more weight that LTF levels (and rbr/ drd zones are more so trend continuation zones and don't usually cause reversals after htf levels have been hit). If this is to be the case, then I would expect my 4hr DP to get blown through and become a retest level for price to use so it can rise to the daily/ 4hr source of the move down to look for shorts (pictured in the second chart.) The other half of me still thinks that we should still get a nice bounce off my sell limit area since it was such a clean strong and untested area of supply. So how would you personally play this Shadow Pit?? The scenarios I see are to wait for ltf confirmation in the 4hr DP since my take profit at htf demand was hit before my order was triggered. Secondly, I could just take my order off and expect it to get blown through and retested on the way up to the daily source. Third, I could leave the order on as planed with the same TP. Last but not least, I could be more aggressive with my trailing stop/ profit taking once the position is triggered by the sell limit or ltf confirmation since the sell trigger initiated after htf demand was hit. Also, do you ever leave limit orders on if your take profit has been hit before the entry got triggered?? SyT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: cuimingzhao 31/43 Find the source, trade it- SyT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=50 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 3 - RTM Community QUICKHROEPMLYE REPELYDUCQAUTOITOEN FORUM MORE LOGOUT THANK YOU Trade Management #72 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 sylntthunder wrote: Hello Shadow Pit, The question that has plagued me for quite some time is should you leave a limit order in place after your TP line has been hit??? .... So how would you personally play this Shadow Pit?? ..... Also, do you ever leave limit orders on if your take profit has been hit before the entry got triggered?? ... Hello SyT. Firstly, good job on running through the various scenarios you could be making, but you're still wondering which scenario you choose will lead to profit? What I think you really should be asking is how do you know what trading decisions to make as well as the when and the why? A lot of this will come down to how disciplined and concious you are with your decision making and knowing (having the PROOF) if those decisions are profitable for you over a large sample size (You've forward tested the sh*t out of your decision making, you have tried various scenarios of the same trades and compared the results). https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=50 32/43 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT At the end of the day you will have to make one decision over another (trade or no trade, limit order or wait n see etc.) and what might be 'right' one day will be 'wrong' the next but one of your key objectives in trading should be to minimize risk so you've got the 'wrong' decisions covered that way at least! Remember.. you simply cannot 'WIN' every single trade regardless of how well prepared you were for all possible scenarios. OK OK ok so I have rambled and I haven't told you anything you don't already know...guess what I probably can't actually help you for this one trade nor decide for you the most effective approach in the future. But here's the part where I make some suggestions anyway. Treat trading like a business right? This is one way to address the dilemma...gather PROOF that one decision is better than the other. If you want to put in the real hard work, FORWARD test 100 trades similar to your USDCAD analysis and compare the stats (equity curve, expectancy etc.): 1. In the 1st account place the limit order, with SL and TP and leave it be. 2. In the 2nd account place a limit order...AFTER the REACTION validates the zone, with SL behind reaction pattern and TP with consideration to price structure of the APPROACH. 3. In the 3rd account duplicate scenario 1. but apply some trade management using the HTF. i.e. trail the stop, what is the reaction, how does this effect the outcome of my SL and TP placement, how is price behaving as it comes close to your sl or tp 4. In the 4th account duplicate scenario 2. but apply some trade management using the HTF. i.e. trail the stop, how does this effect the outcome of my SL and TP placement, how is price behaving as it comes close to your sl or tp 5. In the 5th account duplicate scenario 2 but instead use a market order where you can comfortably define your risk and reward 6. ADD all the scenarios you're interested in understanding how it effects your longer term performance ...I think you get the picture..You're testing combinations of decisions and seeing which ones are the most effective and which ones are useless! By doing this, not only will you learn a thing or two about reading the market but you have now collected high quality reliable EVIDENCE, reflective of YOUR decision making and you will know where YOUR strengths and weaknesses are in regards to https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=50 33/43 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 3 - RTM Community applying each scenario and you will know which scenarios you will want to continue HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT forward with. Essentially, you're evolving your own trading decision tree/process map/flow chart. You're eliminating unreliable trading decisions and focusing your future attention on improving profitable decisions. Rinse and repeat. Eventually you will be 5 years deep in your career and all that hard work and the knowledge you have gained about your trading decisions will be called 'experience', and you will instinctively know what decisions to make and when. Trading is a lot of hard work. Let's revisit your USDCAD dilemma, assuming it aligns with all the RTM teachings... except of course for the fact that you're committing to a trade decision before you've even witnessed the approach or the reaction. Maybe you just leave the limit order with SL & TP, maybe you are right and it's a perfect trade, but ask yourself, what does that really mean in the grand scheme of things, do I rule out all the other scenarios because this one time this strategy worked? Of course not, you want the BEST trade execution & trade management strategies that work for you, the only way to find that out is to measure them against each other, and choose what works for you. p.s. I personally wouldn't have a limit order here anyway, because as you have established it has indeed invalidated the original analysis and price is now coming FROM a place of evident demand and the supply to stop its run is yet to be determined (this could be a rule in your trading plan like 'too much uncertainty for a limit order'). I would instead have an alert 10 pips before the zone, and be interested in watching the APPROACH and the REACTION as it happens. This usually provides a lower risk entry anyway or perhaps keeps you out of the trade altogether. So in response to your last question, no I would delete the order and get excited about watching the right of the chart unfold. p.p.s. We are in a new week and month so don't forget to look at the WEEKLY/MONTHLY chart as well They all have targets too. How many times have you watched price react to something and gone, 'oh boy I wish I traded that when I noticed it..'? To be honest I didn't think for too long when I executed, I saw a setup (H1 selling) and I dropped down to the LTF and participated with a limit order, with reasons based on probabilities (more likely vs less likely) and potential expectancy (is there room to run!?). I suspect you are a little interested in some deeper thought process, so let's try and 'unpack' what I might have thought in the moment. Let's highlight reasons to justify it. What did I see? What boxes did I mentally try and tick off for this setup? 1. You have price consolidation (range) 2. It is consolidating in a 'good' spot on the HTF 3. With a break in the anticipated direction and an accompanying pole, 4. This pole engulfs the bottom or top of another consolidated area (an FTR, a FL, a DBR, RBD, etc) (not at the time of entry.) 5. The entry is placed at some sort of FL, FTR, DBD/RBR that formed near where the break of the consolidated area happened (a retest) https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=100 25/30 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT So we can very quickly assess that I ticked off 1, 3 & 5. There is no need to think twice about entry. But let's address your potential concerns with what I have just presented: You might argue that with criteria 2 that price/reaction wasn't at a good spot because it hadn't actually traded in the HTF supply zone, but have you looked at a weekly or a monthly chart lately, which way is price going? Maybe that doesn't help so much, so let's be practical... draw a line through 1.4250 and look at it on the daily...zoom it out a little, let's not split hairs about 5-6 pips, this is a GOOD spot for LTF PA to form, previous support becomes resistance. I didn't speculate on this, price signaled its intention when it was ready to go and I had a place to define a setup, with setting the risk and places to look for profit pending future PA. Which leads us onto your other concern...that demand below. Why didn't I wait for criteria 4 to happen before looking for entry? Well, I already spotted profit potential https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=100 26/30 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 5 - RTM Community (room to run!) before it did engulf the next barrier, and the most recent momentum was HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT down as the H1 printed.. I would gladly lose 4-5 trades at 6 pips for a shot at an immediate 4:1 or in this case 15:1. But in reality it's never going to get to that many loses on one setup, because by 2-3 losses I should know that I am wrong or wake up to the fact that I am failing some very basic entry discipline! So for illustration purposes only and on one chart (H4) only. This is for a really simplistic view of how you can be pretty mechanical about how you might read the market on any given day Area 1 of this chart is completely off limits unless Zone A is engulfed. Area 2 of this chart is completely off limits unless Zone B is engulfed. Area 3 (our target) of this chart is completely off limits unless Zone C is engulfed. So with all that in mind you can pretty much conclude and create a single trade plan of action that to realise the Target price must first:... Engulf Zone B ...AND... Engulf Zone C... AND.... NOT Engulf Zone A.. That's a lot of things that need to happen. AND the picture is going to bloody change on https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=150 2/43 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 7 - RTM Community its way there (or on its way away!) AND price will create new zones within the same HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT range! eep..It just takes a while for it all to sink in to our brains to act upon..so as they say...practice makes perfect :) So hold up a second! The Day is still young and there will be new PA printed in 4 hours, 8 hours, 12, 16..20 that you will need to incorporate into your read of this price chart and add it to the plan! AND you still need to include this same logic to multiple time frames to give yourself the best chances of reading intention correctly.. I guarantee that once you do this a few times over you will eventually realise that its not just a simple matter of saying ONE FL engulf on ONE timeframe has automatically opened up some overhead target (because if you resign yourself to that statement of truth you will ignore everything else that's in front of you that tells you the complete opposite, which is the ultimate thinking trap)...now...let us go have a look at Ifmyante's post here and ponder why its just easier to take most of our profit at tg1 i.e. your immediate obstacles Blackhawkfx wrote: Hi ShadowPit, need your opinion. Something I picked up on another forum which I try to incorporate into my trading, is adding and removing lots to my position, so that after I get an initial movement in my direction, I can keep my average position beyond a "safe" extreme. When my trade arrives at my first target, I will remove say half the lots, and then if it looks like the RT from my target is failing and the move is going to continue, you can put them back on again (or even more), such that your average position is always kept at B/E or better. It's just going to take time. So! Give yourself a break from time to time, the subconscious works in mysterious ways. Another way you can learn is to try trading without a stop (put an OH shit! stop for safety) for a while and exit manually where you would normally put your stop. This forces you to continue thinking about the market reaction at your predefined level and proactively make a decision on when to exit, when to hold, when to move your stop back in. It buys you time to think about what price is doing, observe HTF, observe correlations, what you might have missed, where it might be targeting, where else it could reverse, why you should or shouldn't stay in the trade and accept the bigger risk. When you get it wrong and price blows straight through, that usually means you have 23/39 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=175 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 8 - RTM Community missed one or two things completely. I've got a growing checklist that fires off in my HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT mind when I'm observing charts, placing trades, deciding what to do next...it only takes a few extra seconds to stop, breathe and think... Does it make sense in the HTF? Does it make sense in the LTF? Where is price coming from/going to in the HTF/LTF? What has price just done on approach? Broken a range? Broken some other FL I ignored? Flagged? Compressed? Finding support/resistance on new price action? Is there evidence of momentum at this level? Is the level even fresh? Time of day? News? Is TG1 far away enough to make this worthwhile? Any correlations to support the strength/weakness of the currency? You're good at this. Find out the reason why something failed, make note in your head and move on. p.s. Have you visited the Price is Everything thread on FF? I find myself going back there every other month to view Reds and Dropped Onions posts through the lens of my new understandings. Observe how they read price, its very iterative, in the moment, baby sitting the trade at every turn. It's a great way to learn to apply your thinking on top of theirs. p.p.s. The trading without a stop suggestion is purely to get you out of the routine of setting and forgetting and into the zone of continuous decision making and understanding the reaction and if its meeting your expectation or not. You can worry about the R:R stuff after you feel confident that you're reading price correctly and consistently. Here's a story (queue violin): when I first got interested in trading, probably much like some of you did, I created spreadsheets which linearly took my meager account deposit from rags to riches in a matter of 50 excel rows! As it turned out, I am not the kind of trader who can string together an infinite amount of wins in a row. So a while ago I created a pretty basic spreadsheet using excels Random number generator, which would model a simple equity curve a little closer to my reality. Stumbled across it again today, made it presentable, thought I would share it. Alternatively, there are also a multitude of modelers when you google equity curve generators. Discipline and patience; something I struggled with from the beginning and still do to some extent. Having something visual, whether it be a trading plan, wallpaper of an expensive car or house or a sexy randomised equity curve helps to remind me that this is a long term game, and that it is actually okay to be WRONG, and have a string of losses, and the importance of getting over losses quickly. It really is amazing what IRON DISCIPLINE will achieve. Food for thought: How long would it realistically take you to execute 1,000 quality trades, and what could your equity curve look like after that type of sample size? https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=175 35/39 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT File Attachment: File Name: EquityCurveModeling.xls File Size: 161 KB Attachments: 36/39 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=175 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: Michal238, Lupy, Ywicked, gembel, Kovalenko, Experiorz, pfs, Rdk123, Meanwhile, Paichayon, Whenever, Jurij, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU ShadowPit #200 Kovalenko Offline Associate Trader Posts: 746 Thank you received: 1886 Hi mate. I intentionally shared the LTF first. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Entry/Exit Strategies 1. One trade strategy for these charts could be to sell from orange zone to red ellipse. 2. Another trade strategy could be to wait for price to reach red ellipse and take a touch trade long and hold for a higher high on the daily. 3. Another trade strategy could be to wait for new LTF demand if it reaches red ellipse Reasons for aborting or refining strategy or creating new strategy a. If price breaks above orange zone strategy 1 is a failure. b. If price breaks below the red zone ellipse, strategy 2 is a failure, but strategy 3 may not have been executed. Strategy 4 would be to look for new supply/FL to form at the ellipse. c. etc. What is the meaning of all this? Highlighting the importance of planning ahead of time. Whether you do this planning in your head, through a written personal journal or just plain marking up charts and questioning the logic; the fundamental point is to extract the most value and profit from trading RTM style, you must continuously evaluate a chart so that you can: - identify obsolete trade strategies and cull them - identify new opportunities that didn't previously exist https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 9/41 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community - cut your losses HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT - hold onto your winners - reevaluate risk - win At time of post submission, it looks like price might penetrate north of the supply zone. EURUSD M1 If you had waited for PA to form at this area, the M1 has produced a cheap and new opportunity that was not present in the original analysis. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 11/41 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Food for thought When it comes to M30/H1 zones. Would you rather risk a full 30 pips for your trade to work out? Or wait for PA to form on LTF and have up to a maximum of 6 goes (6 x 5 pips = 30 pips) at the same trade idea? (I am willing to bet the market will not give you 6 opportunities on the LTF at the same zone using RTM methodology) What if the first opportunity works out on the LTF. Instead of risking your full R of 30 pips. You only risk 1/6th (5 pips). If you only risk 1% per trade idea (30 pips in this example), then all you have risked is .16% of your account. We really do have a lot of control on how we profit from the market. If you can't control the final outcome over the distance a trade idea can go, at least control what you sacrifice as collateral should the trade idea not go according to plan. Sound. Risk. Management. Ever wondered why when people post their positive equity curves they don't go at a 45 degree angle? It's because it takes LOTS and LOTS of attempts at seemingly inconspicuous trade ideas with low risk, and potential for high reward. You will experience, lots of small losers/breakevens, lots of small wins, a few big wins. The few big wins are what will point your equity curve upward. /rant over https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 12/41 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Ramjet, Ywicked, Experiorz, charlespips, Paichayon, gizzo, ashiki, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU AUDJPY 18-Sep #206 ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 13/41 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community AUDJPHYOMM5 E EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Ywicked, Experiorz, Paichayon, Whenever, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU AUDJPY Follow up #207 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 14/41 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 Almost there Attachments: 15/41 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: gembel, Kovalenko, Experiorz, Paichayon, HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU AUDJPY Follow up #208 gembel Away Associate Trader Posts: 1549 Thank you received: 1430 ShadowPit wrote: Almost there hi mate can i ask something.. 16/41 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2084-shadowpit?start=200 7/27/2018 ShadowPit - Page 9 - RTM Community your last trade on AJ..
